🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

ഒരു ബാങ്ക് ചെക്കിൽ ഒപ്പിട്ട് നൽകുന്നത് പോലെ — ഒപ്പ് മാറ്റാനോ, തുക തിരുത്താനോ, ഒപ്പിട്ടത് ഞാനല്ലെന്ന് പറയാനോ കഴിയില്ല!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: MOBILE-Q32

Classify and explain security attacks in mobile computing into static and dynamic attacks on mobile assets. Mobile Computing-ile Security Attacks: Passive vs Active (Static vs Dynamic) classify cheyyuka. മൊബൈൽ നെറ്റ്‌വർക്കിലെ സെക്യൂരിറ്റി അറ്റാക്കുകൾ: പാസീവ് vs ആക്ടീവ് അറ്റാക്കുകൾ തരംതിരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
📌 Remember This
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage

Static attacks eavesdrop/monitor without modifying data (Passive); Dynamic attacks intercept, alter, delete, or inject fake data (Active/Man-in-the-Middle).

📌 ⚡ Attack Taxonomy
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\text{Passive / Static: Eavesdropping, Sniffing (No data change); Active / Dynamic: Replay, Man-in-the-Middle, Spoofing, DoS}$$

Ormavekkan: Passive attacks detect cheyyan paadanu (encryption vazhi prevent cheyyanam); Active attacks easily detect cheyyam (tampering, fake sender, server crashing).

📌 ⚡ അറ്റാക്ക് വർഗ്ഗീകരണം
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\text{പാസീവ് (ഒളിഞ്ഞു കേൾക്കൽ) } \quad \text{vs} \quad \text{ആക്ടീവ് (ഡാറ്റ മാറ്റിയെഴുതൽ, DoS)}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: പാസീവ് അറ്റാക്കിൽ ഡാറ്റ മാറ്റമില്ലാതെ ചോർത്തുന്നു; ആക്ടീവ് അറ്റാക്കിൽ വ്യാജ സന്ദേശങ്ങളും മാറ്റങ്ങളും വരുത്തുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

Security attacks targeting mobile communication environments are classified into two major categories:

Attack Category Nature of Threat Primary Examples & Impact
Static (Passive) Attacks The attacker monitors, sniffs, and listens to traffic without altering the data or interrupting the connection. Undetectable by the sender/receiver.
  • Eavesdropping / Packet Sniffing: Intercepting unencrypted Wi-Fi packets using Wireshark.
  • Traffic Analysis: Monitoring transmission frequency and packet size to deduce user habits or location.
Dynamic (Active) Attacks The attacker actively modifies, tampers with, deletes, replays, or injects fake packets into the data stream, disrupting normal service.
  • Man-in-the-Middle (MitM): Rogue Access Point ("Evil Twin") intercepting and altering banking traffic.
  • Denial of Service (DoS / Jamming): Flooding cellular towers with RF noise to knock phones offline.
  • Replay Attack: Capturing a valid authentication token and retransmitting it later to gain unauthorized entry.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Passive Attacks (Eavesdropping):

  • Hacker packets intercept cheythu monitor cheyyunnu without altering data content.
  • Eg: Wi-Fi packet sniffing (Wireshark), traffic pattern analysis, IMSI catching. Prevention: Strong encryption (WPA3, TLS).

2. Active Attacks (Modification & Impersonation):

  • Masquerading / Spoofing: Attacker pretends to be legitimate base station or router (Rogue AP / Evil Twin).
  • Replay Attack: Valid authentication message record cheythu pinnedu reuse cheythu unauthorized login undakkunnu.
  • Message Modification: Changing bank transaction amount or destination account number.
  • Denial of Service (DoS): Radio jamming or flooding server to crash wireless connection.

Security attacks targeting mobile communication environments are classified into two major categories:

Attack Category Nature of Threat Primary Examples & Impact
Static (Passive) Attacks The attacker monitors, sniffs, and listens to traffic without altering the data or interrupting the connection. Undetectable by the sender/receiver.
  • Eavesdropping / Packet Sniffing: Intercepting unencrypted Wi-Fi packets using Wireshark.
  • Traffic Analysis: Monitoring transmission frequency and packet size to deduce user habits or location.
Dynamic (Active) Attacks The attacker actively modifies, tampers with, deletes, replays, or injects fake packets into the data stream, disrupting normal service.
  • Man-in-the-Middle (MitM): Rogue Access Point ("Evil Twin") intercepting and altering banking traffic.
  • Denial of Service (DoS / Jamming): Flooding cellular towers with RF noise to knock phones offline.
  • Replay Attack: Capturing a valid authentication token and retransmitting it later to gain unauthorized entry.

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: MOBILE-Q33

Explain SSL/TLS protocol architecture and discuss its adaptation in mobile networks including WTLS and Mobile VPNs. Mobile networks-ile Security Protocols: SSL, TLS, and Mobile VPN detail aayi explain cheyyuka. സുരക്ഷാ പ്രോട്ടോക്കോളുകൾ: SSL, TLS, മൊബൈൽ വിപിഎൻ (VPN) എന്നിവ വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
📌 Remember This
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage

TLS encrypts transport layer (HTTPS); Mobile VPN (IPsec/WireGuard) creates encrypted IP tunnel that survives IP address changes during mobile roaming.

📌 ⚡ Secure Transport Protocols
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\text{TLS 1.3: Asymmetric Handshake (ECDHE) } \to \text{ Symmetric Session Key (AES-GCM)} \to \text{Encrypted Tunnel}$$

Ormavekkan: SSL obsolete aayi, TLS (Transport Layer Security) aanu modern standard (HTTPS). Mobile VPN (IPsec / WireGuard) cellular roaming-il IP address maarumbol polum secure encrypted tunnel intact aakki nirthunnu.

📌 ⚡ സുരക്ഷിത പ്രോട്ടോക്കോളുകൾ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\text{HTTPS = HTTP + TLS എൻക്രിപ്ഷൻ ടണൽ}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഡാറ്റയെ പബ്ലിക് ഇന്റർനെറ്റിലൂടെ ആർക്കും ചോർത്താൻ കഴിയാത്ത സുരക്ഷിത തുരങ്കം വഴി കടത്തിവിടുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. SSL / TLS Architecture:

  • Transport Layer Security (TLS 1.3, superseding obsolete SSL) operates above TCP to provide end-to-end encrypted sessions (HTTPS).
  • Comprises two sub-protocols:
    • TLS Handshake Protocol: Uses asymmetric cryptography (RSA / Elliptic Curve Diffie-Hellman) to authenticate server identity via X.509 certificates and negotiate a shared symmetric session key.
    • TLS Record Protocol: Encrypts payload data using fast symmetric ciphers (AES-GCM / ChaCha20) and verifies message integrity via HMAC.

2. Mobile Virtual Private Network (Mobile VPN):

  • A standard VPN drops connection whenever a mobile phone switches from Wi-Fi to 4G (because the client's local IP address changes).
  • A Mobile VPN (using IPsec with MOBIKE / IKEv2 or WireGuard) maintains a persistent virtual IP tunnel. When the physical IP address changes, the Mobile VPN updates the tunnel endpoint seamlessly without interrupting enterprise database sessions or forcing the user to log in again.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide
  1. SSL / TLS Architecture: Operates between Application Layer (HTTP) and Transport Layer (TCP).
    • TLS Handshake Protocol: Server authenticates using X.509 certificate. Both parties agree on cipher suite and generate symmetric session key using Diffie-Hellman.
    • TLS Record Protocol: Encrypts application payloads using AES-GCM and verifies HMAC integrity.
  2. Mobile VPN (MOBIKE / IPsec): Traditional VPN network maarumbol disconnect aavum. Mobile VPN uses MOBIKE (IKEv2 Mobility) to seamlessly switch tunnels between Wi-Fi and 5G without re-login!
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. SSL / TLS Architecture:

  • Transport Layer Security (TLS 1.3, superseding obsolete SSL) operates above TCP to provide end-to-end encrypted sessions (HTTPS).
  • Comprises two sub-protocols:
    • TLS Handshake Protocol: Uses asymmetric cryptography (RSA / Elliptic Curve Diffie-Hellman) to authenticate server identity via X.509 certificates and negotiate a shared symmetric session key.
    • TLS Record Protocol: Encrypts payload data using fast symmetric ciphers (AES-GCM / ChaCha20) and verifies message integrity via HMAC.

2. Mobile Virtual Private Network (Mobile VPN):

  • A standard VPN drops connection whenever a mobile phone switches from Wi-Fi to 4G (because the client's local IP address changes).
  • A Mobile VPN (using IPsec with MOBIKE / IKEv2 or WireGuard) maintains a persistent virtual IP tunnel. When the physical IP address changes, the Mobile VPN updates the tunnel endpoint seamlessly without interrupting enterprise database sessions or forcing the user to log in again.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

TLS ഹാൻഡ്‌ഷേക്ക് ഘട്ടങ്ങളും മൊബൈൽ വിപിഎൻ റോമിംഗ് ഗുണങ്ങളും വിശദീകരിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. SSL / TLS Architecture:

  • Transport Layer Security (TLS 1.3, superseding obsolete SSL) operates above TCP to provide end-to-end encrypted sessions (HTTPS).
  • Comprises two sub-protocols:
    • TLS Handshake Protocol: Uses asymmetric cryptography (RSA / Elliptic Curve Diffie-Hellman) to authenticate server identity via X.509 certificates and negotiate a shared symmetric session key.
    • TLS Record Protocol: Encrypts payload data using fast symmetric ciphers (AES-GCM / ChaCha20) and verifies message integrity via HMAC.

2. Mobile Virtual Private Network (Mobile VPN):

  • A standard VPN drops connection whenever a mobile phone switches from Wi-Fi to 4G (because the client's local IP address changes).
  • A Mobile VPN (using IPsec with MOBIKE / IKEv2 or WireGuard) maintains a persistent virtual IP tunnel. When the physical IP address changes, the Mobile VPN updates the tunnel endpoint seamlessly without interrupting enterprise database sessions or forcing the user to log in again.

When you connect to free public Wi-Fi at a railway station, anyone nearby could snoop on your internet packets. TLS (HTTPS) puts your web browsing into sealed envelopes. A Mobile VPN takes it a step further: it creates an armored underground tunnel from your phone all the way to your office computer, shielding all your apps!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Answer / Derivation ID: MOBILE-Q34

Explain the RSA public-key cryptosystem. Solve a complete step-by-step numerical problem for key generation, encryption, and decryption. RSA Algorithm step-by-step working explain cheythu numerical solve cheyyuka ($p=3, q=11, e=7$). RSA അൽഗോരിതം വിശദീകരിച്ച് ഉദാഹരണ കണക്ക് ചെയ്യുക ($p=3, q=11, e=7$)

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$n = p \cdot q, \quad \phi(n) = (p-1)(q-1), \quad e \cdot d \equiv 1 \pmod{\phi(n)}, \quad C = M^e \pmod n, \quad M = C^d \pmod n$$

Public key $(e, n)$ encrypts; Private key $(d, n)$ decrypts; security rests on prime factorization hardness of $n$.

📌 ⚡ RSA Formula & Keys
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$n = p \cdot q, \quad \phi(n) = (p-1)(q-1), \quad e \cdot d \equiv 1 \pmod{\phi(n)}, \quad C = M^e \bmod n, \quad M = C^d \bmod n$$

Ormavekkan: Public key $(e, n)$ — anyone can encrypt; Private key $(d, n)$ — only owner can decrypt! Large prime factorization difficulty aanu RSA-yude unbreakable security.

📌 ⚡ RSA സമവാക്യങ്ങൾ
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$n = p \cdot q, \quad C = M^e \bmod n, \quad M = C^d \bmod n$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: പബ്ലിക് കീ $(e, n)$ ഉപയോഗിച്ച് ആർക്കും എൻക്രിപ്റ്റ് ചെയ്യാം; പ്രൈവറ്റ് കീ $(d, n)$ ഉള്ളയാൾക്ക് മാത്രമേ ഡീക്രിപ്റ്റ് ചെയ്യാൻ സാധിക്കൂ.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. RSA Algorithm Mathematical Framework:

  1. Choose two large distinct prime numbers $p$ and $q$.
  2. Calculate modulus $n = p \cdot q$.
  3. Calculate Euler's totient function $\phi(n) = (p - 1)(q - 1)$.
  4. Select an encryption public exponent $e$ such that $1 < e < \phi(n)$ and $\gcd(e, \phi(n)) = 1$.
  5. Calculate private decryption key $d$ using Extended Euclidean algorithm: $$d \cdot e \equiv 1 \pmod{\phi(n)} \implies d \equiv e^{-1} \pmod{\phi(n)}$$
  6. Encryption: For plaintext message $M < n$, ciphertext is $C = M^e \pmod n$.
  7. Decryption: Plaintext is recovered via $M = C^d \pmod n$.

2. Complete Step-by-Step Numerical Example:
Let prime numbers be $p = 3$ and $q = 11$, and message plaintext $M = 2$.

  • Step 1: Compute $n$
    $$n = p \cdot q = 3 \times 11 = 33$$
  • Step 2: Compute $\phi(n)$
    $$\phi(n) = (p - 1)(q - 1) = (3 - 1)(11 - 1) = 2 \times 10 = 20$$
  • Step 3: Select Public Key $e$
    Choose $e = 3$. Check: $1 < 3 < 20$ and $\gcd(3, 20) = 1$. (Valid)
  • Step 4: Compute Private Key $d$
    Solve $3 \cdot d \equiv 1 \pmod{20} \implies 3d = 20k + 1$:
    For $k = 1$: $3d = 21 \implies d = 7$. (Valid)
    Public Key: $(e=3, n=33)$, Private Key: $(d=7, n=33)$.
  • Step 5: Encryption
    $$C = M^e \pmod n = 2^3 \pmod{33} = 8 \pmod{33} = 8$$ The encrypted ciphertext is $C = 8$.
  • Step 6: Decryption (Verification)
    $$M = C^d \pmod n = 8^7 \pmod{33}$$ Using modular exponentiation: $$8^2 = 64 \equiv -2 \pmod{33}$$ $$8^4 = (-2)^2 = 4 \pmod{33}$$ $$8^7 = 8^4 \times 8^2 \times 8^1 = 4 \times (-2) \times 8 = -64 \equiv 2 \pmod{33}$$ The original message $M = 2$ is recovered correctly.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Numerical Solution

Step-by-Step Calculation:

  1. Choose two prime numbers: $p = 3, \;\; q = 11$.
  2. Compute modulus: $n = p \cdot q = 3 \times 11 = 33$.
  3. Compute Euler totient: $\phi(n) = (p - 1)(q - 1) = (3 - 1)(11 - 1) = 2 \times 10 = 20$.
  4. Choose public exponent $e = 7$ (check $\gcd(7, 20) = 1$, valid!).
  5. Calculate private key $d$: $e \cdot d \equiv 1 \pmod{\phi(n)} \implies 7d \equiv 1 \pmod{20}$.
    Multiples of 20: $21, 41, 61 \dots$ Notice that $7 \times 3 = 21 = 1 \pmod{20}$.
    $\therefore d = 3$!
  6. Encryption of Message $M = 2$: $$C = M^e \bmod n = 2^7 \bmod 33 = 128 \bmod 33 = 29$$ $\therefore$ Ciphertext $C = 29$!
  7. Decryption of Ciphertext $C = 29$: $$M = C^d \bmod n = 29^3 \bmod 33$$ $29 \equiv -4 \pmod{33} \implies 29^3 \equiv (-4)^3 = -64 \pmod{33}$. $-64 + 2 \times 33 = -64 + 66 = 2$. $\therefore$ Decrypted Message $M = 2$! (Verified 100% correct).
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. RSA Algorithm Mathematical Framework:

  1. Choose two large distinct prime numbers $p$ and $q$.
  2. Calculate modulus $n = p \cdot q$.
  3. Calculate Euler's totient function $\phi(n) = (p - 1)(q - 1)$.
  4. Select an encryption public exponent $e$ such that $1 < e < \phi(n)$ and $\gcd(e, \phi(n)) = 1$.
  5. Calculate private decryption key $d$ using Extended Euclidean algorithm: $$d \cdot e \equiv 1 \pmod{\phi(n)} \implies d \equiv e^{-1} \pmod{\phi(n)}$$
  6. Encryption: For plaintext message $M < n$, ciphertext is $C = M^e \pmod n$.
  7. Decryption: Plaintext is recovered via $M = C^d \pmod n$.

2. Complete Step-by-Step Numerical Example:
Let prime numbers be $p = 3$ and $q = 11$, and message plaintext $M = 2$.

  • Step 1: Compute $n$
    $$n = p \cdot q = 3 \times 11 = 33$$
  • Step 2: Compute $\phi(n)$
    $$\phi(n) = (p - 1)(q - 1) = (3 - 1)(11 - 1) = 2 \times 10 = 20$$
  • Step 3: Select Public Key $e$
    Choose $e = 3$. Check: $1 < 3 < 20$ and $\gcd(3, 20) = 1$. (Valid)
  • Step 4: Compute Private Key $d$
    Solve $3 \cdot d \equiv 1 \pmod{20} \implies 3d = 20k + 1$:
    For $k = 1$: $3d = 21 \implies d = 7$. (Valid)
    Public Key: $(e=3, n=33)$, Private Key: $(d=7, n=33)$.
  • Step 5: Encryption
    $$C = M^e \pmod n = 2^3 \pmod{33} = 8 \pmod{33} = 8$$ The encrypted ciphertext is $C = 8$.
  • Step 6: Decryption (Verification)
    $$M = C^d \pmod n = 8^7 \pmod{33}$$ Using modular exponentiation: $$8^2 = 64 \equiv -2 \pmod{33}$$ $$8^4 = (-2)^2 = 4 \pmod{33}$$ $$8^7 = 8^4 \times 8^2 \times 8^1 = 4 \times (-2) \times 8 = -64 \equiv 2 \pmod{33}$$ The original message $M = 2$ is recovered correctly.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം കണക്കുകൂട്ടൽ

$p=3, q=11 \implies n=33, \phi(n)=20$. $e=7$ ആകുമ്പോൾ $d=3$. സന്ദേശം $M=2$ ആകുമ്പോൾ $C = 2^7 \bmod 33 = 29$. തിരികെ $M = 29^3 \bmod 33 = 2$ എന്ന് തെളിയിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

  6. Encryption: For plaintext message $M < n$, ciphertext is $C = M^e \pmod n$.
  7. Decryption: Plaintext is recovered via $M = C^d \pmod n$.

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Imagine a padlock that has an open position and a special key:

Anyone can click the padlock shut ($e$)—that is the Public Key. You can distribute thousands of open padlocks to anyone in the world. Anyone can put a secret message into a box and snap your lock shut. But only you hold the unique brass key ($d$) that can unlock it—that is the Private Key.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Padlock and Key analogy: Public key $(e, n)$ oru thuranna padlock aanu — lokathil aarkku venamenkilum aa lock vechu box poottam (Encryption). Pakshe aa lock thurakkan ulla orotta key $(d, n)$ ningalude kayyil mathrame ullu (Decryption)!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

തുറന്ന പൂട്ട് പബ്ലിക് കീയും, അത് തുറക്കാനുള്ള ഒരേയൊരു താക്കോൽ പ്രൈവറ്റ് കീയും ആയ അത്ഭുത ഗണിതശാസ്ത്ര വിദ്യ!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Answer / Derivation ID: MOBILE-Q35

Derive the Diffie-Hellman key exchange algorithm. Explain the discrete logarithm problem and discuss vulnerability to Man-in-the-Middle attack. Diffie-Hellman Key Exchange Algorithm derivation-um numerical example-um explain cheyyuka. ഡിഫി-ഹെൽമാൻ കീ എക്സ്ചേഞ്ച് അൽഗോരിതവും കണക്കുകൂട്ടലും വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$A = g^a \pmod p, \quad B = g^b \pmod p, \quad K = B^a \pmod p = A^b \pmod p = g^{ab} \pmod p$$

Alice and Bob establish a shared secret $K$ over an insecure public channel without transmitting secrets $a$ or $b$.

📌 ⚡ Diffie-Hellman Shared Secret
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$K = (Y_B)^{X_A} \bmod q = (Y_A)^{X_B} \bmod q = \alpha^{X_A X_B} \bmod q$$

Ormavekkan: Alice-um Bob-um insecure public channel vazhi secret share cheyyathe thanne ore shared symmetric key $K$ calculate cheyyunnu! Discrete Logarithm Problem solve cheyyan mathamatically kazhiyilla ennullathaanu security.

📌 ⚡ പങ്കിട്ട രഹസ്യ കീ
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$K = Y_B^{X_A} \bmod q = Y_A^{X_B} \bmod q$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: രഹസ്യ കീ പുറത്തുവിടാതെ തന്നെ രണ്ട് വ്യക്തികൾക്ക് ഒരേ പൊതു കീ ഉണ്ടാക്കിയെടുക്കാൻ സഹായിക്കുന്ന രീതി.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Mathematical Foundation:
Diffie-Hellman key exchange enables two communicating parties (Alice and Bob) to establish a shared symmetric secret key over an unencrypted public radio link. Its security relies on the computational difficulty of the Discrete Logarithm Problem (DLP): given large prime $p$, generator $g$, and $Y = g^X \pmod p$, it is computationally infeasible to compute the secret exponent $X$ in polynomial time.

2. Step-by-Step Protocol Execution:

  1. Public Parameter Agreement: Alice and Bob publicly agree on a large prime modulus $p$ and a primitive root generator $g$ ($g < p$). An eavesdropper (Eve) can see both $p$ and $g$.
  2. Secret Exponent Selection:
    • Alice chooses a secret private integer $a$ ($1 < a < p$).
    • Bob chooses a secret private integer $b$ ($1 < b < p$).
  3. Public Key Computation and Exchange:
    • Alice computes public key $A = g^a \pmod p$ and transmits $A$ over the channel to Bob.
    • Bob computes public key $B = g^b \pmod p$ and transmits $B$ over the channel to Alice.
  4. Shared Secret Key Calculation:
    • Alice computes the shared key: $K = B^a \pmod p = (g^b)^a \pmod p = g^{ab} \pmod p$.
    • Bob computes the shared key: $K = A^b \pmod p = (g^a)^b \pmod p = g^{ab} \pmod p$.
  5. Both parties possess the identical shared secret session key $K = g^{ab} \pmod p$.

3. Numerical Example:
Let public parameters be prime $p = 23$, generator $g = 5$.

  • Alice picks private $a = 6 \implies A = 5^6 \pmod{23} = 15625 \pmod{23} = 8$. (Sends $A=8$)
  • Bob picks private $b = 15 \implies B = 5^{15} \pmod{23} = 19$. (Sends $B=19$)
  • Alice computes: $K = B^a \pmod p = 19^6 \pmod{23} = 2$.
  • Bob computes: $K = A^b \pmod p = 8^{15} \pmod{23} = 2$.
  • Shared Secret Key: $K = 2$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Step-by-Step Guide

1. Global Public Parameters: Prime $q = 11$, Primitive root $\alpha = 2$.

2. Alice: Selects secret private key $X_A = 3$. Calculates public key: $$Y_A = \alpha^{X_A} \bmod q = 2^3 \bmod 11 = 8$$ Sends $Y_A = 8$ across public network to Bob.

3. Bob: Selects secret private key $X_B = 4$. Calculates public key: $$Y_B = \alpha^{X_B} \bmod q = 2^4 \bmod 11 = 16 \bmod 11 = 5$$ Sends $Y_B = 5$ across public network to Alice.

4. Shared Secret Key Calculation:
Alice computes: $K = (Y_B)^{X_A} \bmod q = 5^3 \bmod 11 = 125 \bmod 11 = 4$.
Bob computes: $K = (Y_A)^{X_B} \bmod q = 8^4 \bmod 11 = 4096 \bmod 11 = 4$.
Both independently obtained identical key $K = 4$ without transmitting it!

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Think of mixing paint colors in plain view of an eavesdropper:

Alice and Bob agree on a common background color (Yellow). Alice secretly adds her private color (Teal) to make a blended muddy color, which she sends to Bob. Bob secretly adds his private color (Orange) to Yellow, creating a different blend, and sends it to Alice. Now, Alice adds her secret Teal to Bob's blend, and Bob adds his secret Orange to Alice's blend. Both end up with the exact same final mixture! An eavesdropper saw all the blended paints travel across the room, but cannot separate the original secret colors.

Eavesdropper-nu intermediate mix kandu secret color theerumanikkan pattilla!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Answer / Derivation ID: MOBILE-Q36

Explain the mathematical principle of Orthogonal Frequency Division Multiplexing (OFDM). Discuss subcarrier orthogonality and Cyclic Prefix (CP). OFDM (Orthogonal Frequency Division Multiplexing) principles-um Cyclic Prefix-um explain cheyyuka. ഒഎഫ്ഡിഎം (OFDM) തത്വങ്ങളും സൈക്ലിക് പ്രിഫിക്സും (Cyclic Prefix) വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\Delta f = \frac{1}{T_s}, \quad \int_0^{T_s} \cos(2\pi f_i t)\cos(2\pi f_j t)dt = 0 \quad (i \ne j)$$

OFDM splits wideband channel into orthogonal narrowband subcarriers; Cyclic Prefix (CP) eliminates Intersymbol Interference (ISI).

📌 ⚡ Orthogonality & Cyclic Prefix
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\Delta f = \frac{1}{T_{\text{sym}}}, \quad \int_0^T \cos(2\pi f_i t)\cos(2\pi f_j t) dt = 0 \quad (i \ne j), \quad T_{CP} > \tau_{\max}$$

Ormavekkan: High-speed serial stream-ne parallel low-speed subcarriers aakki split cheyyunnu. Subcarriers mathematically orthogonal aayathukond spectrum overlap cheythaalum zero interference! Cyclic prefix ISI (Inter-Symbol Interference) completely eliminate cheyyunnu (4G LTE, 5G, Wi-Fi 6).

📌 ⚡ OFDM തത്വം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\Delta f = \frac{1}{T_{sym}} \implies \text{ഇന്റർ-സിംബൽ ഇന്റർഫെറൻസ് (ISI) ഒഴിവാക്കുന്നു}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഒരു വലിയ ഡാറ്റയെ നിരവധി ചെറിയ സബ്-കാരിയറുകളാക്കി മാറ്റി സ്പെക്ട്രം കാര്യക്ഷമമായി ഉപയോഗിക്കുന്നു (4G, 5G, Wi-Fi 6).

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Motivation for OFDM:
In high-speed wireless networks (4G LTE, 5G NR, Wi-Fi 6), transmitting data at high symbol rates over a single carrier causes the symbol duration $T_s$ to become smaller than the channel multipath delay spread $T_d$ ($T_s < T_d$). This produces severe frequency-selective fading and destructive Intersymbol Interference (ISI).

2. Core Mathematical Principle of Orthogonality:
OFDM splits a single high-speed serial data stream into $N$ parallel low-speed substreams modulated onto closely spaced subcarriers. The subcarrier frequency spacing is chosen to be: $$\Delta f = \frac{1}{T_s}$$ Over symbol period $T_s$, the subcarriers are mathematically orthogonal: $$\frac{1}{T_s} \int_0^{T_s} e^{j 2\pi f_i t} \cdot e^{-j 2\pi f_j t} \, dt = \begin{cases} 1, & i = j \\ 0, & i \ne j \end{cases}$$ Because subcarriers are orthogonal, their spectral lobes overlap heavily without causing cross-talk! This yields maximum spectral efficiency without needing guard bands between subcarriers.

3. Role of the Cyclic Prefix (CP):

  • To completely eliminate Intersymbol Interference caused by multipath echoes from buildings, a guard interval is inserted before each OFDM symbol.
  • Instead of leaving the guard interval empty (which would destroy subcarrier orthogonality), the end portion of the OFDM symbol is copied and pasted onto the beginning of the symbol. This is called the Cyclic Prefix (CP).
  • As long as the cyclic prefix length exceeds the channel delay spread ($T_{\text{CP}} > T_{\text{delay}}$), multipath echoes fall harmlessly inside the CP window, preserving zero ISI and zero Inter-Carrier Interference (ICI).

4. Generation via IFFT/FFT:
Modulation is performed digitally using an Inverse Fast Fourier Transform (IFFT) at the transmitter, and demodulation is executed using an FFT at the receiver, making hardware implementation highly efficient.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Orthogonal Subcarriers: Peak of one subcarrier occurs exactly at the zero-crossings of all other subcarriers ($\Delta f = 1/T_s$). No guard bands required, maximizing spectral efficiency!

2. FFT / IFFT Implementation: Transmitter uses IFFT to convert frequency symbols into time domain; receiver uses FFT to decode.

3. Cyclic Prefix (CP): End of the OFDM symbol is copied and pasted to the beginning. As long as $T_{CP} > \text{multipath delay spread } \tau_{\max}$, multipath reflections from tall buildings will fall inside CP and won't corrupt the main data symbol — zero Inter-Symbol Interference (ISI)!

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Motivation for OFDM:
In high-speed wireless networks (4G LTE, 5G NR, Wi-Fi 6), transmitting data at high symbol rates over a single carrier causes the symbol duration $T_s$ to become smaller than the channel multipath delay spread $T_d$ ($T_s < T_d$). This produces severe frequency-selective fading and destructive Intersymbol Interference (ISI).

2. Core Mathematical Principle of Orthogonality:

This yields maximum spectral efficiency without needing guard bands between subcarriers.

  • As long as the cyclic prefix length exceeds the channel delay spread ($T_{\text{CP}} > T_{\text{delay}}$), multipath echoes fall harmlessly inside the CP window, preserving zero ISI and zero Inter-Carrier Interference (ICI).

4. Generation via IFFT/FFT:
Modulation is performed digitally using an Inverse Fast Fourier Transform (IFFT) at the transmitter, and demodulation is executed using an FFT at the receiver, making hardware implementation highly efficient.

ഓർത്തോഗൊണാലിറ്റി കണ്ടീഷൻ, IFFT/FFT പ്രോസസ്സിംഗ്, സൈക്ലിക് പ്രിഫിക്സ് ISI തടയുന്ന വിധം എന്നിവ വിശദീകരിക്കുക.

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Imagine transporting 100 passengers across a busy bridge. If you put everyone into one gigantic, long trailer truck (single carrier), it easily gets jammed and delayed around tight corners (ISI). But if you put the 100 passengers into 50 small parallel cars (OFDM subcarriers) driving side by side, each car moves at a relaxed, safe pace and arrives without getting stuck!

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy

Oru valiya heavy load oru single big lorry-il kondupoyaal narrow road-il traffic block aavum (Single carrier ISI). Athinu pakaram 100 small auto-rickshaws-il aakki parallel aayi ayachaal (OFDM parallel subcarriers), oru road block aayalum delivery smooth aayi nadakkum! 4G, 5G, Wi-Fi ellaam ee auto-rickshaw fleet pole aanu!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Answer / Derivation ID: MOBILE-Q37

Explain Multimedia Broadcast and Multicast Services (MBMS) in cellular networks and discuss MPLS label switching in mobile core backbones. MBMS (Multimedia Broadcast Multicast Service) and MPLS in mobile cellular networks explain cheyyuka. മൾട്ടിമീഡിയ ബ്രോഡ്കാസ്റ്റ് മൾട്ടികാസ്റ്റ് സർവ്വീസും (MBMS) MPLS-ഉം വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage

MBMS transmits one single radio stream to unlimited users simultaneously (Point-to-Multipoint); MPLS uses 32-bit labels for wire-speed core packet forwarding.

📌 ⚡ Single Transmission for Millions
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\text{Unicast: } N \text{ copies}; \quad \text{Multicast (MBMS): } 1 \text{ single radio broadcast copy for } N \text{ users!}$$

Ormavekkan: Live cricket match stadium-il ulla 50,000 aalukal kaanumbol: Unicast aaneel tower collapse aavum! MBMS single radio stream broadcast cheythu ella phone-ilum deliver cheyyunnu. MPLS 20-bit short labels vechu core network-il ultra-fast routing tharunnu.

📌 ⚡ ബ്രോഡ്കാസ്റ്റ് കാര്യക്ഷമത
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\text{ഒറ്റ ട്രാൻസ്മിഷൻ വഴി ഒരേസമയം ലക്ഷക്കണക്കിന് ആളുകൾക്ക് തത്സമയ സ്ട്രീമിംഗ്}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: തത്സമയ ക്രിക്കറ്റ് മത്സരങ്ങളും വാർത്തകളും നെറ്റ്‌വർക്ക് ജാമാവാതെ എല്ലാവരിലേക്കും എത്തിക്കാൻ MBMS സഹായിക്കുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Multimedia Broadcast Multicast Service (MBMS):

  • Problem: If 1,000 mobile subscribers in a stadium stream a live football match via standard unicast, the mobile tower must transmit 1,000 separate radio streams, instantly crashing the cellular network.
  • MBMS Solution: MBMS transmits a single broadcast radio bearer over the cell. All 1,000 subscribers listen to that single radio stream simultaneously (Point-to-Multipoint), conserving $99.9\%$ of radio spectrum!
  • Core Architecture:
    • BM-SC (Broadcast Multicast Service Center): Central server authenticating broadcast content providers and scheduling content streams.
    • MBMS Gateway: Replicates IP multicast packets to serving base stations.

2. Multi-Protocol Label Switching (MPLS) in Mobile Backhaul:

  • Traditional IP routing inspects destination IP addresses at every single hop, causing router latency bottlenecks.
  • MPLS Principle: Packets entering the mobile core network are stamped with a short 32-bit Label. Core routers (Label Switch Routers, LSRs) inspect only this label and forward packets along pre-established Label Switched Paths (LSP) using fast hardware lookups.
  • Benefits for Mobile Networks:
    • Enables deterministic Quality of Service (QoS) and traffic engineering for latency-sensitive 5G voice and video streams.
    • Enables rapid network protection switching ($< 50\text{ ms}$ rerouting if a fiber link breaks).

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. MBMS Architecture: BM-SC (Broadcast Multicast Service Center) schedules live stream. Single packet stream flows from GGSN/PGW to cell towers. Over the air, all handsets in the cell tune to the same shared radio channel.

2. MPLS (Multiprotocol Label Switching): Router long IP headers (32-bit/128-bit) complex routing table lookup cheyyunnathinu pakaram, simple 20-bit numeric labels swap cheythu high-speed hardware forwarding nadathunnu. Guarantees QoS and traffic engineering in mobile backhaul.

    • MBMS Gateway: Replicates IP multicast packets to serving base stations.

2. Multi-Protocol Label Switching (MPLS) in Mobile Backhaul:

  • Traditional IP routing inspects destination IP addresses at every single hop, causing router latency bottlenecks.
  • MPLS Principle: Packets entering the mobile core network are stamped with a short 32-bit Label. Core routers (Label Switch Routers, LSRs) inspect only this label and forward packets along pre-established Label Switched Paths (LSP) using fast hardware lookups.

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

MBMS: Instead of having 50 separate televisions in a sports bar each playing their own private stream of the football game, you put one big screen on the wall that all 50 people watch together!

MPLS: Instead of postal workers reading the full destination country, state, city, street, and house address on every single letter at every street corner, they stamp a simple 2-digit color tag on the envelope so sorting machines zip it along at lightning speed!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Answer / Derivation ID: MOBILE-Q38

Compare the architectural and functional evolution of cellular mobile communication networks from 2G to 5G. Cellular Network Generations: 2G muthal 5G vareyulla architectural and performance comparison explain cheyyuka. സെല്ലുലാർ നെറ്റ്‌വർക്ക് പരിണാമം: 2G മുതൽ 5G വരെയുള്ള മാറ്റങ്ങൾ താരതമ്യം ചെയ്യുക

Watch Video Solution
📌 Remember This
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage

2G (Voice/TDMA, 64 kbps) $\to$ 3G (CDMA/WCDMA, 2 Mbps) $\to$ 4G (All-IP/OFDMA, 100 Mbps) $\to$ 5G (Network Slicing, eMBB/URLLC/mMTC, 10-20 Gbps).

📌 ⚡ 2G to 5G Evolution Metric
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\text{2G: 64 kbps (Voice)} \to \text{3G: 2 Mbps (Data)} \to \text{4G: 100 Mbps (All-IP)} \to \text{5G: 20 Gbps, } <1\text{ ms (eMBB, URLLC, mMTC)}$$

Ormavekkan: 2G Circuit switched TDMA voice; 3G WCDMA mobile internet; 4G All-IP LTE OFDM (Flat architecture, EPC); 5G Service-Based Architecture (SBA), Network Slicing, Massive MIMO, and mmWave.

📌 ⚡ തലമുറകളുടെ പരിണാമം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\text{2G (വോയ്സ്) } \to \text{ 3G (വെബ്) } \to \text{ 4G (വീഡിയോ) } \to \text{ 5G (അതിവേഗം, IoT, റോബോട്ടിക്സ്)}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: വേഗത കിലോബൈറ്റിൽ നിന്നും ഗിഗാബൈറ്റിലേക്കും, ലേറ്റൻസി മില്ലിസെക്കൻഡിൽ താഴേക്കും കുറഞ്ഞ സാങ്കേതിക കുതിപ്പ്.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

Mobile cellular communications evolved across five generations to meet surging data demands:

Generation Primary Access & Core Architecture Peak Data Speed Key Technical Features & Applications
2G
(GSM, 1991)		 TDMA / FDMA.
Circuit-switched core (MSC, HLR, VLR).		 $9.6\text{ to } 64\text{ kbps}$
(GPRS: $171\text{ kbps}$)		 First digital cellular system. Introduced digital encrypted voice, SMS text messaging, and SIM cards.
3G
(UMTS, 2001)		 W-CDMA / CDMA2000.
Dual-domain core (Circuit-switched voice + Packet-switched data).		 $2\text{ to } 42\text{ Mbps}$
(HSPA+)		 True mobile broadband; enabled mobile web browsing, video calling, mobile TV, and GPS navigation.
4G LTE
(2010)		 OFDMA (Downlink) + SC-FDMA (Uplink).
Flat All-IP EPC (Evolved Packet Core: MME, SGW, PGW).		 $100\text{ Mbps to } 1\text{ Gbps}$
(LTE-Advanced)		 Eliminated circuit switching entirely (Voice over LTE / VoLTE); enabled HD video streaming, smartphone app economy, and low latency ($< 20\text{ ms}$).
5G NR
(2020)		 Scalable OFDMA (Sub-6 GHz & mmWave).
Service-Based Architecture (SBA) with Network Slicing and Cloud-native microservices.		 $10\text{ to } 20\text{ Gbps}$ Three core pillars:
  1. eMBB: Enhanced Mobile Broadband (ultra-fast downloads, AR/VR).
  2. URLLC: Ultra-Reliable Low-Latency Communication ($< 1\text{ ms}$, autonomous driving, remote surgery).
  3. mMTC: Massive Machine-Type Communication ($10^6 \text{ IoT devices/km}^2$).

2G (GSM)TDMA / FDMA$64\text{ kbps}$$300\text{--}500\text{ ms}$Circuit Switched (MSC, HLR, VLR)Digital voice calls, SMS
3G (UMTS)W-CDMA$2\text{ Mbps}$$100\text{--}150\text{ ms}$Dual (Circuit voice + Packet GPRS)Mobile web browsing, video calls
4G (LTE)OFDMA + SC-FDMA$100\text{--}1000\text{ Mbps}$$20\text{--}40\text{ ms}$All-IP Flat Evolved Packet Core (EPC)HD video streaming, VoLTE
5G (NR)Scalable OFDMA + Massive MIMO$10\text{--}20\text{ Gbps}$$<1\text{ ms}$Cloud-Native Service Based Architecture (SBA)eMBB, URLLC (remote surgery), mMTC (IoT)

Mobile cellular communications evolved across five generations to meet surging data demands:

Generation Primary Access & Core Architecture Peak Data Speed Key Technical Features & Applications
2G
(GSM, 1991)		 TDMA / FDMA.
Circuit-switched core (MSC, HLR, VLR).		 $9.6\text{ to } 64\text{ kbps}$
(GPRS: $171\text{ kbps}$)		 First digital cellular system. Introduced digital encrypted voice, SMS text messaging, and SIM cards.
3G
(UMTS, 2001)		 W-CDMA / CDMA2000.
Dual-domain core (Circuit-switched voice + Packet-switched data).		 $2\text{ to } 42\text{ Mbps}$
(HSPA+)		 True mobile broadband; enabled mobile web browsing, video calling, mobile TV, and GPS navigation.
4G LTE
(2010)		 OFDMA (Downlink) + SC-FDMA (Uplink).
Flat All-IP EPC (Evolved Packet Core: MME, SGW, PGW).		 $100\text{ Mbps to } 1\text{ Gbps}$
(LTE-Advanced)		 Eliminated circuit switching entirely (Voice over LTE / VoLTE); enabled HD video streaming, smartphone app economy, and low latency ($< 20\text{ ms}$).
5G NR
(2020)		 Scalable OFDMA (Sub-6 GHz & mmWave).
Service-Based Architecture (SBA) with Network Slicing and Cloud-native microservices.		 $10\text{ to } 20\text{ Gbps}$ Three core pillars:
  1. eMBB: Enhanced Mobile Broadband (ultra-fast downloads, AR/VR).
  2. URLLC: Ultra-Reliable Low-Latency Communication ($< 1\text{ ms}$, autonomous driving, remote surgery).
  3. mMTC: Massive Machine-Type Communication ($10^6 \text{ IoT devices/km}^2$).

🇮🇳 മലയാളം താരതമ്യം

ടേബിളിൽ സ്പീഡ്, ലേറ്റൻസി, പ്രോട്ടോക്കോൾ, ആർക്കിടെക്ചർ, സേവനങ്ങൾ എന്നിവ വ്യക്തമായി താരതമ്യം ചെയ്യുക.

Mobile cellular communications evolved across five generations to meet surging data demands:

Generation Primary Access & Core Architecture Peak Data Speed Key Technical Features & Applications
2G
(GSM, 1991)		 TDMA / FDMA.
Circuit-switched core (MSC, HLR, VLR).		 $9.6\text{ to } 64\text{ kbps}$
(GPRS: $171\text{ kbps}$)		 First digital cellular system. Introduced digital encrypted voice, SMS text messaging, and SIM cards.
3G
(UMTS, 2001)		 W-CDMA / CDMA2000.
Dual-domain core (Circuit-switched voice + Packet-switched data).		 $2\text{ to } 42\text{ Mbps}$
(HSPA+)		 True mobile broadband; enabled mobile web browsing, video calling, mobile TV, and GPS navigation.
4G LTE
(2010)		 OFDMA (Downlink) + SC-FDMA (Uplink).
Flat All-IP EPC (Evolved Packet Core: MME, SGW, PGW).		 $100\text{ Mbps to } 1\text{ Gbps}$
(LTE-Advanced)		 Eliminated circuit switching entirely (Voice over LTE / VoLTE); enabled HD video streaming, smartphone app economy, and low latency ($< 20\text{ ms}$).
5G NR
(2020)		 Scalable OFDMA (Sub-6 GHz & mmWave).
Service-Based Architecture (SBA) with Network Slicing and Cloud-native microservices.		 $10\text{ to } 20\text{ Gbps}$ Three core pillars:
  1. eMBB: Enhanced Mobile Broadband (ultra-fast downloads, AR/VR).
  2. URLLC: Ultra-Reliable Low-Latency Communication ($< 1\text{ ms}$, autonomous driving, remote surgery).
  3. mMTC: Massive Machine-Type Communication ($10^6 \text{ IoT devices/km}^2$).

Figure: Figure 5.8: Generational Evolution of Cellular Telephony from 2G GSM to Cloud-Native 5G Service-Based Architecture

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

The generations of mobile networks are like vehicle upgrades on a highway:

  • 2G: A telegram delivery bicycle (reliable voice and text).
  • 3G: A small family hatchback car (can load web pages and simple music).
  • 4G: A high-speed electric sports car (smoothly streams 4K YouTube and video calls anywhere).
  • 5G: A supersonic bullet train network with dedicated private tracks: one track for ultra-fast entertainment, one dedicated hyper-safe track for driverless cars, and one track monitoring millions of smart city meters!
💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Vandi speed evolution pole aanu: 2G oru cycle pole (basic SMS/Voice). 3G oru scooter pole (photos and email). 4G oru sports car pole (YouTube HD video streaming). 5G oru rocket pole (ultra-low latency, self-driving autonomous cars, robotic remote surgery, and smart cities)!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

സൈക്കിളിൽ (2G) നിന്ന് തുടങ്ങി റോക്കറ്റ് വേഗതയിലേക്ക് (5G) കുതിച്ചുയർന്ന മൊബൈൽ സാങ്കേതിക വിപ്ലവം!
